115年 10月賦稅收入初步統計 
一、整體賦稅收入 
（一）實徵淨額 
10 月實徵淨額 5,669 億元，較上年同月增加 1,495 億元；累計 1 至
10 月實徵 4 兆 1,383 億元，較上年同期增加 8,499 億元(+25.8%)，
主要由證交稅、營所稅及營業稅所貢獻。隨新版財政收支劃分法實
施，營業稅劃歸中央政府比率由 61.2%降為 4.5%(獲配稅額隨之減
少 2,662 億元)，致中央政府稅收增加數 3,906 億元(+15.8%)，不及
地方政府增加數 4,671 億元(+68.5%)。 
（二）預算達成率 
累 計1 至 10 月 實 徵 淨 額 占 全 年 預 算 數 91.4% ， 其 中 中 央 政 府 為
92.8%，地方政府為87.7%；與全年預算數差距分別為3,913億元、
2,227億元、1,614億元。 
圖 1 賦稅實徵淨額及年增率

說明：115 年 5-7 月年增率跳動較大，係因 114 年所得稅結算申報及繳納期限延長至 6 月底所致。

二、主要稅目別變動
（一）10 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以營利事業所得稅增加 1,133 億元、
證券交易稅增加 220 億元及綜合所得稅增加 120 億元較多。
（二）累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 3,297
億元、營利事業所得稅增加 2,986 億元及營業稅增加 1,272 億元較多。
圖 2 主要稅目別變化
(115 年 1-10 月)
1.營利事業所得稅
(1)10 月實徵淨額 4,144 億元，較上年同月增加 1,133 億元(+37.6%)，
主因暫繳稅款成長。
(2)累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 1 兆 4,172 億元，較上年同期增加 2,986
億元(+26.7%)，主因結算申報自繳、暫繳稅款俱增及延分期稅款
陸續入帳。
2.綜合所得稅
10 月實徵淨額 614 億元，較上年同月增加 120 億元(+24.3%)，主因
扣繳稅款增加所致；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 8,894 億元，較上年同
期增加 910 億元(+11.4%)，主因結算申報自繳稅額增加，以及盈餘分

配、薪資所得等扣繳稅款均呈成長。
3.營業稅
(1)10月受進口代徵稅額增加及適用外銷零稅率營業人申請退稅增加
之交互影響，營業稅實徵淨額為負77億元，較上年同月(負146億元)
增加69億元。
(2)累 計1 至10 月 實 徵 淨額6,153 億 元 ， 較 上年 同 期 增 加1,272 億元
(+26.1%)，主因內需穩定，資通與視聽產品、電子零組件等進口
代徵稅額增加，惟外銷營業人申請退稅亦增，抵銷部分增幅。

4.貨物稅
10月實徵淨額73億元，較上年同月減少41億元(-35.6%)，累計1至10
月實徵淨額948億元，較上年同期減少248億元(-20.7%)，主因新購汽
機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增加，加以汽柴油減徵幅度擴大所致。
5.證券交易稅
(1)10 月及累計 1 至 10 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 1,210
億元、1 兆 1,437 億元，分別較上年同期(6,267 億元、4,632 億元)
增 78.9%、1.5 倍。
(2)10 月證交稅實徵淨額 523 億元， 較上年同月增加 220 億元
(+72.6%)；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 5,583 億元，較上年同期增加
3,297 億元(+1.4 倍)。
6.關稅
10 月實徵淨額 132 億元，較上年同月增加 7 億元(+6.0%)，累計 1 至
10 月實徵淨額 1,356 億元，較上年同期增加 88 億元(+6.9%)，均因機
械及電機設備、基本金屬、塑橡膠及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
10 月實徵淨額 23 億元，較上年同月減少 11 億元(-31.9%)，主因大額

稅款案件減少；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 234 億元，較上年同期增加
32 億元(+15.9%)，主因大額稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
10 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 3 億元(+5.9%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 560 億元，較上年同期減少
11 億元(-2.0%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
9.使用牌照稅
10 月實徵淨額 6 億元，較上年同月減少 1 億元(-10.2%)，累計 1 至
10 月實徵淨額 676 億元，較上年同期減少 1 億元(-0.1%)，主因開徵
輛數減少，以及稅款入帳時間差異所致。
（三）前 10月主要稅目實徵淨額占全年預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%(已提前達成全年預算目標)者有遺產稅(123%)、贈與稅
(107%)、印花稅(106%)、房屋稅(105%)及營所稅(102%)。
2.低於 100%者有牌照稅(99%)、營業稅(94%)、綜所稅(93%)、契稅
(90%)、關稅(88%)、菸酒稅(81%)、證交稅(80%)、貨物稅(79%)、土

增稅(71%)及特銷稅(47%)等。

三、政府別賦稅收入概況
(一)中央政府
累計 1 至 10 月中央政府賦稅收入(不含中央統籌分配稅款)實徵淨
額 2 兆 8,579 億元(占全年預算數 92.8%)，較上年同期增加 3,906 億元
(+15.8%)，主因新版財政收支劃分法施行，營業稅劃歸中央政府比率
大幅調降，獲配稅額隨之減少 2,662 億元，惟證交稅、所得稅分別增加
3,297 億元、3,306 億元，互抵後呈現上升。
(二)地方政府
累計 1 至 10 月地方政府賦稅
收入(含中央統籌分配稅款)實
徵淨額 1 兆 1,487 億元(占全年
預算數 87.7%)，較上年同期增
加 4,671 億元(+68.5%)，主因
納入中央統籌分配之營業稅
增加 4,013 億元所致。
(三)中央特種基金
累計 1 至 10 月撥入中央特種基金之健康福利捐實徵淨額 220 億元，較
上年同期增加 4 億元(+1.7%)；房地合一課徵所得稅撥入住宅基金 144
億元，較上年同期減少 6 億元(-4.0%)；撥入長照基金 953 億元(其中 575

億元來自房地合一課徵所得稅、220 億元來自菸稅，158 億元來自遺贈
稅)，較上年同期增加 2 億元(+0.2%)。

Document 063: 2026年10月宏观数据分析：出口高位运行、PPI涨幅扩大，内需依然偏弱

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月宏观数据分析：出口高位运行、PPI涨幅扩大，内需依然偏弱.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 15
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
研究员：万亮 邮箱：xnqh_wl@swfutures.com
期货从业证书号：F03116714 交易咨询从业证书号：Z0019298
时间：2026.11.17
出口高位运行、PPI 涨幅扩大，内需依然偏弱
——2026 年10 月宏观数据分析

### Page 2

2
我们梳理了 2026 年 10月国内重要的宏观经济数据，以增加对当前宏观经
济运行节奏的理解。
总的来说，2026年 10月国内经济延续了外需强、内需弱，生产转弱、需
求不足的格局。10月制造业采购经理指数回落至 49.3%，重回收缩区间；工业
增加值、社会消费品零售总额增速同步放缓，固定资产投资和房地产销售降幅
进一步扩大；与此同时，进出口维持高增，PPI同比涨幅扩大至 5.7%，M1 增
速明显回升，外需与物价的表现明显好于内需。
对此，我们认为，需要理性客观的看待当前宏观经济 K型分化形势。新经
济保持高景气度，但尚不足以对冲房地产和基建等传统经济的下行，国内经济
的复苏不能一蹴而就。当前外部不稳定不确定因素较多，国内有效需求不足，
经济运行面临不少挑战。这要求实施更加积极有为的宏观政策，持续扩大内需、
优化供给，推动经济实现质的有效提升和量的合理增长。当前房地产市场处在
企稳复苏的关键节点，后续对宏观经济的拖累有望显著收窄。整体而言，2026
年面临的宏观环境好于 2025 年，但强劲复苏的可能性不大。

一、制造业 PMI 重回收缩区间
10 月份，制造业采购经理指数（PMI）为 49.3%，比上月下降 0.8 个百分
点，重回收缩区间。从企业规模看，大型企业 PMI 为 50.2%，比上月下降 0.4
个百分点，仍高于临界点；中、小型企业 PMI分别为 48.4%和 47.5%，比上月
下降 1.3个和 1.4个百分点，均低于临界点。
从分类指数看，构成制造业 PMI的 5个分类指数中，仅供应商配送时间指
数高于临界点，生产指数、新订单指数、原材料库存指数和从业人员指数均低
于临界点。生产指数为 49.8%，比上月下降 1.9 个百分点，表明制造业生产活

### Page 3

3
动有所放缓。新订单指数为 49.4%，比上月下降 1.1 个百分点，表明制造业市
场需求景气度下降。原材料库存指数为 48.4%，比上月上升 0.2 个百分点，表
明制造业主要原材料库存量降幅有所收窄。从业人员指数为 48.4%，与上月持
平，表明制造业企业用工景气度总体平稳。供应商配送时间指数为 50.2%，比
上月上升 0.1个百分点，表明制造业原材料供应商交货时间加快。
10月份，非制造业商务活动指数为 49.7%，比上月下降 0.5个百分点，非
制造业景气水平有所下降。分行业看，建筑业商务活动指数为 48.0%，比上月
下降 2.3个百分点；服务业商务活动指数为 50.0%，比上月下降 0.2个百分点。
建筑业新订单指数降至 40.0%，比上月下降 5.7 个百分点，是本月非制造业景
气回落的主要拖累。
总的来说，10月制造业 PMI环比下降 0.8个百分点，重回收缩区间。其中
生产指数大幅回落 1.9 个百分点至 49.8%，新订单指数降至 49.4%，需求不足
对生产的制约进一步显现。大型企业 PMI仍在扩张区间，中、小型企业则加速

收缩，企业规模之间的景气分化有所扩大。非制造业商务活动指数同步回落至
49.7%，建筑业新订单指数降至 40.0%，稳投资政策的效果尚未在景气指标中体
图 1：中国制造业 PMI
（数据来源：ifind 资讯）

### Page 4

现。
二、10月 CPI 同比上涨 0.7%，PPI 同比上涨 5.7%
2026年 10月份，全国居民消费价格同比上涨 0.7%。其中，城市上涨 0.7%，
农村上涨 0.7%；食品价格下降 2.9%，非食品价格上涨 1.5%；消费品价格上涨
0.8%，服务价格上涨 0.7%。扣除食品和能源价格的核心 CPI 同比上涨 0.8%，
涨幅比上月回落 0.2个百分点。
10月份，全国居民消费价格环比持平。其中，城市持平，农村下降 0.1%；
食品价格下降 1.0%，非食品价格上涨 0.2%；消费品价格下降 0.1%，服务价格
上涨 0.1%。
10月份，食品烟酒及在外餐饮类价格同比下降 1.6%，影响 CPI下降约 0.48
个百分点。食品中，鲜菜价格下降 16.9%，影响 CPI下降约 0.31 个百分点；畜
肉类价格下降 2.7%，影响 CPI下降约 0.11个百分点，其中猪肉价格下降 8.6%；
鲜果价格下降 3.8%，影响 CPI 下降约 0.07 个百分点；蛋类价格上涨 10.1%，

影响 CPI上涨约 0.05 个百分点。其他七大类价格同比六涨一降。其中，交通通
信、医疗保健、其他用品及服务价格分别上涨 5.5%、2.4%和 1.8%，教育文化
娱乐、衣着、生活用品及服务价格分别上涨 1.5%、1.1%和 0.2%；居住价格下
降 0.3%。

### Page 5

5
2026年 10月份，全国工业生产者出厂价格同比上涨 5.7%，环比上涨 0.7%。
工业生产者购进价格同比上涨 7.9%，环比上涨 1.1%。其中，煤炭开采和洗选
业、石油和天然气开采业价格同比分别上涨 36.6%和 35.0%，石油煤炭及其他
燃料加工业、有色金属冶炼和压延加工业、化学原料和化学制品制造业价格同
比分别上涨 29.9%、18.6%和 16.4%，对 PPI形成较大拉动；计算机通信和其他
电子设备制造业价格同比上涨 5.9%，AI 投资的高景气度仍在带动电子行业价
格回升。生产资料价格同比上涨 7.4%，生活资料价格下降 0.1%，上下游价格
分化依然显著。
总的来说，2026 年物价指数的回升，主要受输入性通胀拉动。10月 PPI同

比涨幅扩大 0.7 个百分点至 5.7%，环比上涨 0.7%，其中采掘工业价格同比上
涨 27.9%、环比上涨 2.8%，能源和化工链条仍是主要拉动。CPI同比涨幅由 0.9%
回落至 0.7%，核心 CPI由 1.0%回落至 0.8%；其中交通工具用能源价格同比涨
幅由 14.9%扩大至 20.2%，是 CPI的主要支撑项，而鲜菜价格同比下降 16.9%、
猪肉价格下降 8.6%，食品端继续形成拖累。CPI与 PPI的剪刀差由 4.1个百分
点扩大至 5.0个百分点，验证了国内产能充足、内需相对不足的局面。
三、出口高位运行，进口明显提速
以美元计，10月，中国进出口总值 7193.8亿美元，同比增长 37.9%；出口
总值 4123.3亿美元，同比增长 35.2%；进口总值 3070.5亿美元，同比增长 41.6%；
贸易顺差 1052.8亿美元。出口增速较上月小幅回落 1.1个百分点，仍处历史高
位；进口增速较上月加快 10.6个百分点，明显提速。
其中，10月，集成电路出口额同比增长 137.1%（前值为 136%）、自动数

据处理设备及其零部件出口额同比增长 114.8%（前值为 92.9%）。国内进出口
均受益于全球 AI 投资的高景气度。以人民币计，10月货物进出口总额 48683
亿元，同比增长 31.2%。
分国别来看，10月中国对美出口 464.14亿美元，同比增速为 32.9%；对欧
图 4：中国进出口增速

### Page 7

7
盟的出口额为 545.81亿美元，出口增速为 24.3%；对东盟国家的出口额为 779.75
亿美元，同比增长 46.3%；对日本的出口金额为 148.67 亿美元，同比增速为
14.3%。对主要贸易伙伴出口均保持两位数正增长。
得益于中国产业强大竞争力和旺盛的海外需求，出口增速维持高位，特别
是 AI投资强相关的计算机、电子和通信行业进出口均保持高增。10月对东盟
出口占出口总额的 18.9%，继续稳居第一大出口市场，出口市场多元化格局进
一步深化。展望未来，中国外贸真正的风险不在于美国关税的增加，而在于美
国经济衰退风险加大，全球经济增速放缓导致的需求下降。预计 2026 年中国
进出口均有望延续高景气态势。

额 1.26万亿元，同比增长 9.0%；委托贷款余额 11.53万亿元，同比增长 1.7%；

### Page 9

9
居民信贷结构上，10月居民贷款减少约 4300 亿元；其中，短期贷款减少
约 2700 亿元，中长期贷款减少 1563 亿元。居民短期与中长期贷款双双净减少，
居民消费和购房信贷需求进一步走弱。
企业信贷结构上，10月企业贷款净减少 1600亿，其中短期贷款增加约 100
亿元，中长期贷款增加约 1600 亿元，票据融资减少 3117亿元。票据融资大幅
压降是拖累企业贷款的主要因素，而企业中长期贷款仍保持小幅增加，表明设
备更新和项目融资需求尚有一定支撑，但整体扩张意愿依然不强。
1-10月政府债券净融资 11.49万亿元，同比少 4592亿元；其中 10月单月
净融资约 1.19 万亿元，同比多增约 7000 亿元，地方政府债务结存限额等增量
政策在 10月集中落地，成为支撑社融的主要力量。1-10月社融存量增速 7.2%，
高于人民币贷款增速 4.7%，两者剪刀差扩大至 2.5个百分点。
10月末，广义货币（M2）余额 359.06万亿元，同比增长 7.1%，增速环比
回落 0.1个百分点。狭义货币（M1）余额 116.9 万亿元，同比增长 4.4%，增速

环比回升 1.2个百分点。M1-M2剪刀差由-4.0%收窄至-2.7%，资金活化程度有
所改善。
总的来说，2026年 10月金融数据呈现总量偏弱、结构分化的特征。人民
币贷款单月净减少，居民贷款全面收缩、企业票据融资大幅压降，反映实体部
门的融资需求依然不振；社融存量增速回升主要依靠政府债券放量，财政扩张
仍是信用扩张的主要来源。M1 增速明显回升、剪刀差收窄，与财政资金集中
拨付和企业活期存款改善有关，但能否传导至实体投资需求仍待观察。

五、社零增速有所回落，固定资产投资降幅扩大

### Page 14

14
2026年 10月房地产销售面积、投资降幅继续扩大，新开工降幅略有收窄。
我们仍然认为，房地产市场的企稳复苏是一个漫长的过程，难以一蹴而就。当
前，房地产市场已处于底部阶段，房地产新开工面积、投资等或已处于长期均
衡水平下。随着基数的下降，后续商品房销售面积、销售额同比增速降幅将逐
步收窄。随着市场预期的转变，本轮房地产下行周期的“市场底”正在出现。
尽管存在巨大的分歧，2026年有望成为房地产市场止跌回稳的关键节点。
积极因素在逐步累积，房地产对宏观经济和资本市场的拖累将显著收窄。
七、总结与展望
总的来说，2026年 10月国内经济延续了外需强、内需弱，生产转弱、需
求不足的格局。前三季度国内生产总值同比增长 4.6%。10月制造业 PMI回落
至 49.3%，重回收缩区间，工业增加值增速放缓至 4.8%，社会消费品零售总额
增速回落至 0.4%，固定资产投资和房地产销售降幅进一步扩大；与此同时，出
口维持 35.2%的高增，进口增速加快至 41.6%，PPI同比涨幅扩大至 5.7%，M1

增速回升至 4.4%，外需与物价的表现明显好于内需。
对此，我们认为，当前外部不稳定不确定因素较多，国内有效需求不足，
经济运行面临不少挑战。这要求实施更加积极有为的宏观政策，持续扩大内需、
优化供给，推动经济实现质的有效提升和量的合理增长。当前房地产市场处在
企稳复苏的关键节点，后续对宏观经济的拖累有望显著收窄。整体而言，2026
年面临的宏观环境好于 2025 年，但国内经济强劲复苏的可能性不大。
跟踪后续政策的落地细节，观察物价端的回升力度，耐心等待宏观经济的
向上信号。

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年 11 月 6 日

1/2 
2026 年・冬のボーナス予測 
～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

○ 民間企業の2026 年冬のボーナス一人当たり支給額を前年比＋3.0％（昨冬同＋2.8％）と予想する
（厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」ベース）。冬のボーナスとしては６年連続の増加となるだろう。中
東情勢の悪化に伴う資源価格上昇が企業活動の重荷となるなかでも企業収益は高水準を維持してい
る。人手不足を背景とした処遇改善の動きも根強く、今冬もボーナスの増加が続くと予想する。

○ 11 月６日に厚生労働省から公表された26 年夏のボーナス（賞与支給事業所における労働者一人平
均賞与額）は、前年比＋5.3％と５年連続で増加し、昨夏の同＋2.9％から伸びも拡大した。また、
賞与を支給していない事業所も含む「全事業所における労働者一人平均賞与額」で見ても同＋
5.9％と増加している。賞与を支給する事業所の割合も前年の 73.4％から74.0％へ上昇しており、
企業の従業員への還元姿勢が強いことがうかがえる。

○ ボーナスの交渉では、春闘時にその年の年間賞与を決定する夏冬型が採用されていることが多い。
2026 年春闘では、月例給与だけでなく一時金でも増額回答が目立った。企業収益が高水準にある
ことに加え、物価高への配慮や人材確保の観点から、賞与を含めた処遇改善を進める企業が多かっ
たとみられる。また、月例給与の引き上げが賞与の算定基礎額を押し上げ、支給月数の増加と相ま
ってボーナス増につながる面もある。夏のボーナス増に続き、冬についてもこうした交渉結果が反
映される形で増加が予想される。

○ 毎期型の企業、あるいは労働組合が存在しない中小・零細企業においては、より直近の収益状況・
業況がボーナスに反映されやすい。もっとも、イラン情勢悪化によるコスト上昇が企業収益の下押
し要因となるなかでも、価格転嫁の進展などを背景として企業収益は総じて底堅く推移している。
利益の水準も高く、従業員への還元余力はなお保たれているとみてよいだろう。

○ 人手不足感が強いことも、ボーナス増加を後押しする。企業にとって人材の確保・定着は引き続き
重要な経営課題となっており、月例給与だけでなく賞与を含めた処遇改善の必要性は高い。物価高
によって家計負担が増加するなか、従業員への還元を求める声も強い。こうした点も今冬のボーナ
ス増加を支える要因となる。

○ 労務行政研究所が９月30 日に公表した調査によれば、東証プライム上場企業 175 社の 2026 年年末

### Page 2

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
2/2 
一時金妥結額は、前年比＋4.2％と５年連続で増加した。製造業が同＋4.3％、非製造業が同＋
3.5％と、いずれもプラスとなっている。大企業中心の調査であり、中小企業やパートタイム労働
者も含む毎月勤労統計とは対象が異なるものの、冬のボーナスも堅調に増加するという見方を裏付
ける結果といえる。

○ 冬のボーナス増加は家計の所得環境にとって好材料であり、個人消費を下支えすることが期待され
る。一方で懸念されるのが物価上昇の影響である。中東情勢悪化に伴うコスト上昇の価格転嫁など
を背景として、今後は物価上昇率が高まる可能性が高く、政府による電気・ガス代支援の効果を織
り込んでも、2027 年初にはＣＰＩコアの前年比上昇率が＋３％台に達することが予想される。物
価上昇による家計の購買力の圧迫が続くなか、ボーナス増加による個人消費の押し上げ効果につい
ては過度な期待は禁物だろう。

2026～2027年度日本経済見通し（2026年 11月）

（℡：050-5474-7490）

日本・国内総生産（ＧＤＰ）成長率予測値

2026年度予測 実質＋０．９％ （前回 ＋１．０％） 
 名目＋３．６％ （前回 ＋３．２％）

2027年度予測 実質＋１．２％ （前回 ＋１．２％） 
 名目＋３．４％ （前回 ＋３．０％）

※前回は 2026年９月８日時点の弊社予測値

○ 実質ＧＤＰ成長率の見通しは 2026年度が＋0.9％（９月時点予測：＋1.0％）、27年度が＋1.2％
（同＋1.2％）である。暦年では 26年が＋0.9％（同＋0.9％）、27年が＋1.0％（同＋1.0％）とな
る。26年７-９月期のＧＤＰ成長率が前回予測時点での想定を下回ったことを反映し、26年度の
成長率見通しを若干下方修正したが、先行きの景気見通しに大きな変更はない。26年度後半は物
価上昇率の再加速が景気の重荷となるものの、企業部門の底堅さが下支えとなり、緩やかな回復
基調は維持されると予想する。また、27年度にはコスト上昇圧力が徐々に和らぐことに加え、食
料品の消費税減税による押し上げもあり、景気の回復感が徐々に強まるとみている。

○ 今回の予測では、2027年４月から２年間、飲食料品にかかる消費税率を１％に引き下げ、あわせ
て中低所得の就業者を対象に年間 6000億円程度の就業者負担軽減支援金を支給することを前提と
している。家計の購買力増大に伴って、27年度の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は0.2％ポイント強押し上げ
られると想定している。また、27年度の消費者物価指数（総合）の前年比上昇率は▲1.4％ポイン
ト、消費者物価指数（生鮮除く総合）は▲1.2％ポイント程度押し下げられるとみられる。

○ 26年７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は前期比年率▲0.1％と、４四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となっ
た。個人消費が減少したほか、住宅投資や公共投資も成長率を押し下げた。ただし、個人消費の
減少には、４-６月期にみられた自動車やエアコンなどの耐久財需要の反動や、８～９月の天候不

### Page 2

2/5 
順といった一時的な要因が大きく影響したとみられる。一方、設備投資は高い伸びとなり、企業
部門の底堅さが確認された。前期まで３四半期連続で比較的高い成長が続いていたことも踏まえ
れば、今回の小幅なマイナス成長を悲観視する必要はない。家計と企業の回復に濃淡はみられる
ものの、日本経済は均してみれば緩やかな回復基調にあると評価できる。

○ 26年10-12月期は前期比年率＋0.6％と、再びプラス成長に戻ると予想する。７-９月期に個人消
費を押し下げた耐久財需要の反動や天候不順といった一時的な要因が和らぐことに加え、名目賃
金の高い伸びや雇用の改善が消費を下支えするとみられる。設備投資についても、高水準の企業
収益や人手不足を背景とした省力化・デジタル関連の投資需要は根強く、増加基調を維持するだ
ろう。輸出も、海外景気の底堅さや世界的なＡＩ関連需要の拡大を背景に、緩やかな増加が期待
できる。個人消費は力強さを欠くものの、設備投資や輸出が下支えとなることで、景気の緩やか
な回復基調は維持されるだろう。

○ 26年度後半については、既往の資源高や原材料費、物流費などの上昇が時間差を伴って川下へ波
及することが、景気の下押し要因となる。食料品や日用品、耐久財など幅広い品目への価格転嫁
が進むことに加え、ガソリン補助金の段階的縮小も物価を押し上げる。こうした物価上昇率の再
加速によって、足元で続いている実質賃金の改善が一服し、家計の実質購買力が再び圧迫される
可能性がある。また、企業にとっても原材料費や物流費などの上昇が収益の重荷となるだろう。

○ もっとも、こうしたコスト上昇による下押しに対して、高水準の企業収益が一定の緩衝材となる
ことに加え、ＡＩ関連需要の拡大が半導体製造装置や電子部品などの輸出・生産、設備投資を押
し上げていることは景気の下支え要因となる。人手不足を背景とした省力化投資やデジタル関連
投資への需要も根強く、設備投資は増加基調を維持するとみられる。また、海外景気の底堅さも
輸出を支えるだろう。個人消費についても、物価高による下押し圧力が強まるものの、高い賃上
げや底堅い雇用環境が支えとなり、腰折れする可能性は低い。家計部門は力強さを欠く一方、企
業部門は堅調さを維持しており、日本経済は緩やかな回復基調を続けると予想する。

○ 27年度については、原油高や原材料費上昇による物価上昇圧力が徐々に減衰することで、景気の
回復感は次第に強まるとみている。26年秋の原油高の影響が時間差を伴って波及するため、年度
前半は物価上昇圧力がなお残るものの、年度後半にかけて価格転嫁の動きは徐々に落ち着くと予
想する。物価上昇率の鈍化によって家計の実質購買力が改善し、高い賃上げもあいまって、個人
消費の回復力が強まるだろう。食料品への消費税減税と就業者負担軽減支援金の支給も消費を押
し上げる要因となる。企業部門では、コスト上昇圧力の緩和によって収益環境が改善することも
支えとなり、省力化やデジタル・ＡＩ関連を中心とした設備投資が増加する見込みである。輸出
についても、海外景気の底堅さやＡＩ関連需要が引き続き下支え要因となる。物価面からの逆風
が和らぐことで、家計と企業の双方で内需の持ち直しが進み、27年度の景気回復の勢いは 26年度

### Page 3

3/5 
よりも強まると予想する。

○ 先行きのリスクとして注意が必要なのは、米国を中心としたＡＩ関連投資の持続性である。米中
間選挙後、州・地方政府によるデータセンター建設への規制強化が進めば、計画の延期や投資コ
ストの増加を通じて、ＡＩ関連投資の拡大ペースが鈍化する可能性がある。加えて、巨額投資の
収益性や資金調達の持続性に対する懸念も強まっている。これまでの米国景気はＡＩ投資の拡大
と株高に支えられてきた面が大きいため、仮にＡＩブームに変調が生じれば、その影響はハイテ
ク業界にとどまらず、米国経済、ひいては世界経済に波及する恐れがある。ＡＩ関連投資が大幅
に減速すれば、日本の半導体製造装置や電子部品などの輸出・生産、設備投資にも悪影響が及ぶ
だろう。もっとも、建設規制の強化とＡＩ需要そのものの減少は別問題である。ＡＩの活用拡大
に伴う計算需要は引き続き極めて強く、大手テクノロジー企業の投資意欲も旺盛である。既に着
工済みのデータセンターや契約済みの設備投資案件も多く、規制の影響を受けて投資先の地域を
変更する動きも考えられる。ＡＩ関連投資の拡大ペースは幾分鈍化する可能性があるものの、失
速するとは考えにくく、先行きも高水準の投資が続くと予想している。

○ 消費者物価指数（生鮮食品除く総合、ＣＰＩコア）は 26年度が＋2.3％（９月時点予測：＋
2.4％）、27年度が＋1.3％（同＋1.1％）と予想する。26年９月のＣＰＩコアは前年比＋2.1％と
９カ月ぶりの＋２％台となったが、年度後半にかけて一段と上昇率が高まる可能性が高い。食料
品価格は足元で鈍化傾向に歯止めがかかりつつあり、日用品でも値上げの動きが明確化してい
る。26年春の資源価格上昇や調達難の影響に加え、包装資材費や物流費などのコスト増が時間差
を伴って川下へ転嫁されることが物価を押し上げる。ＡＩ需要の拡大や円安の影響による耐久財
価格の上昇も続くとみられる。また、既往の燃料価格上昇は電気・ガス料金に波及するほか、ガ
ソリン補助金の段階的縮小も押し上げ要因となる。なお、冬場の電気・ガス料金補助は一定の押
し下げ要因となるが、前年比への影響は限定的とみられる。企業の価格転嫁姿勢が積極化してい
ることもあり、27年初にかけてＣＰＩコアは前年比＋３％を上回る可能性が高い。

○ 27年度については、26年秋の原油高の影響が時間差を伴って川下へ波及するため、年度前半は物
価上昇圧力が根強く残るとみられる。もっとも、原油高や原材料費上昇によるコストプッシュ圧
力は次第に弱まり、消費税要因を除いても、年度後半にかけて物価上昇率は緩やかに鈍化してい
くと予想する。食料品や日用品、耐久財などへの価格転嫁の動きも次第に落ち着き、物価面から
の家計への下押し圧力が和らぐことで、個人消費の持ち直しが後押しされるだろう。一方、高い
賃上げに伴う人件費上昇や人手不足、企業の積極的な価格設定姿勢は続くとみられ、サービス価
格を中心とした基調的な物価上昇圧力は維持されるとみている。なお、今回の予測では、27年４
月から食料品の消費税率を８％から１％に引き下げることを前提としており、これにより 27年度
のＣＰＩコア上昇率が約1.2％ポイント押し下げられると想定している。

### Page 4

4/5 
○ 金融政策について、日銀は 26年12月の金融政策決定会合で政策金利を1.25％から1.50％に引き
上げると予想する。日銀は 10月会合で、基調的な物価上昇率が２％目標と概ね整合的な水準に達
したとの判断を示し、金融政策運営の重点は「２％への到達」から「２％での定着」へと移っ
た。今後は基調的な物価上昇率を２％程度で安定させることが重要になるが、物価上振れリスク
が残るなか、現在の金融環境はなお緩和的であり、日銀は中立金利に向けて金融緩和の度合いを
縮小していくと考えられる。

○ その後も27年３月に1.75％、６月に2.00％へと、３カ月程度の間隔で追加利上げを行うと予想
する。27年度にはコスト要因による物価上昇圧力が徐々に弱まるとみられるものの、賃金上昇や
人手不足を背景とした基調的な物価上昇圧力は維持されるだろう。物価上振れへの警戒が残るな
か、日銀は段階的な利上げを継続すると予想する。なお、今回の利上げ局面の最終到達点（ター
ミナルレート）は2.0％と見込むが、中立金利の水準には大きな不確実性がある。基調的な物価上
昇率が想定以上に上振れれば 2.0％を超える利上げもあり得るだろう。一方、景気の下振れや既往
の利上げの累積的な影響が想定以上に強まる場合には、利上げペースが鈍化する可能性もある。

股價相對大盤走勢

漢唐(2404 TT) Buy

3Q26 獲利優於預期，客戶建廠需求暢旺，給 Buy 的投資建
議。 
投資建議： 3Q26 獲利優於預期，加上在手訂單金額約 1,939 億
元，有助於未來漢唐營運，給 Buy 的投資建議。 
漢唐 3Q26 獲利優於預期：漢唐 3Q26 營收 275.06 億元，
QoQ+5.47%；稅後純益 45.69 億元，QoQ+21.43%；稅 後 EPS 23.97
元。3Q26 稅後純益優於原先預估的 39.27 億元，主因毛利率優
於預期。 
漢唐目前在手訂單金額約 1,939 億元：漢唐 01~05/2026 年接單
合約金額約 674 億元，截至 05/2026 年已簽約未認列之金額(在
手訂單)約 1,939 億元。在手訂單約 24~36 個月可以認列大部分。
目前在手訂單金額的施工地區，有 2/3 在台灣，1/3 在海外。 
24~36 個月認列大部分的在手訂單可能是保守估計，2H26 在手
訂單金額可能還會再增加。 
漢唐對營運樂觀：漢唐表示目前建廠需求遠大於供給，因目前
人力短缺，尤其是在台灣，暫時沒辦法去接其他大型客戶的訂
單。由於 AI 與相關需求，現在客戶建廠的需求會延續到 2029
年，漢唐對整體產業的展望表示樂觀。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG：  
永續環境是漢唐經營理念之綠色承諾，因應全球環保節能的需求，我們透過
工程設計、系統整合提升工程效率，減少工程過程中能耗。漢唐集成響應政
府綠色採購辦法，我們從原物料取得、工程施作、運輸配送、完工至客戶使
用階段，優先要求廠商提供低污染、可回收、再利用之環保產品，以達到廢
棄物減量之目標。 
漢唐集成根著台灣，從初創之施工安裝小公司成長為專業工程集成公司，有
賴於各地政通人和，因此，漢唐集成本著感恩的心回饋在地，不斷捐助弱勢
及支持培育英才，期望肩擔起企業之「社會責任」，讓台灣變得更美好。漢
唐股東會由全體股東組成，對公司重大事項進行決策，定期聽取董事會報
告，為公司最高決策機關；董事會為最高治理機關，董事會成員皆恪盡善良
管理人之注意義務，擘劃公司的經營政策與檢視財務績效，並確保公司營運
遵守各種法令；為完善公司治理運作、強化公司競爭力，董事會設置審計委
員會與薪資報酬委員會來健全董事會運作；董事會之下亦設有獨立之稽核
室，定期執行稽核業務並向審計委員會與董事會呈報稽核結果。 
公司簡介： 
漢唐集成成立於 1982 年，為一以「全面性整合服務」方式，專業提供高科
技產業建廠之系統集成服務公司；主要業務範圍包括高科技廠房各系統，包
括電腦、控制、無塵室、機電、電信系統之施作與整合服務。 
漢唐目前在手訂單金額約 1,939 億元： 
漢唐 01~05/2026 年接單合約金額約 674 億元，截至 05/2026 年已簽約未認
列之金額(在手訂單)約 1,939 億元。在手訂單約 24~36 個月可以認列大部
分。目前在手訂單金額的施工地區，有 2/3 在台灣，1/3 在海外。 
24~36 個月認列大部分的在手訂單可能是保守估計，2H26 在手訂單金額可
能還會再增加。 
漢唐未來還有先進封裝廠的訂單： 
大客戶於美國建廠的第三期工程的訂單目前漢唐已經有接到一部份，還沒
有認列到手訂單金額中，客戶的先進封裝廠的訂單還沒發包。第二期工程進
度希望在 2026 年認列約 85%，第三期工程的認列狀況要看客戶，未來還有
先進封裝廠的訂單。 
美國營收已超過台灣： 
漢唐沒有透露前兩大客戶的營收占比。2025 年營收占比_地區別：台灣 65%、
美國 25%、其他 9%。 1Q26 台灣 40%、美國 56%、其他 4%。美系記憶體廠
在台灣蓋無塵室，漢唐還是主要的供應商。 
漢唐對營運樂觀： 
漢唐表示目前建廠需求遠大於供給，因目前人力短缺，尤其是在台灣，暫時
沒辦法去接其他大型客戶的訂單。由於 AI 與相關需求，現在客戶建廠的需
求會延續到 2029 年，漢唐對整體產業的展望表示樂觀。

3 群益投顧 
其他補充： 
漢唐的費用比較固定，管理費用包含員工酬勞。漢唐追求與客戶長期合作，
如果是原物料上漲導致成本增加，客戶可以接受漲價。 
目前漢唐在全球的員工人數約 1,700 人，美國、台灣都會再增加人力，之後
也是要看下包廠商，看是否能擴大供應鏈。

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Hon Hai Precision (2317.TW)

3
CPO: New growth opportunities from vertical integration — Hon Hai sees
significant long-term opportunities in both ASIC and vertical integration including
optical interconnect technologies. In optical networking, management believes the
transition from electrical to optical interconnects is structurally inevitable as AI
models expand and memory-bandwidth requirements increase. Hon Hai’s CPO

optical switching already starts mass volume production for its major customer,
and its micron-level precision manufacturing expertise in optical alignment,
connectors, and electrical-to-optical conversion could create additional value
through component integration and higher system-level content. Its extensive
engineering resources, ODM/JDM development capabilities, and vertically
integrated manufacturing platform should strengthen its competitive positioning.

Management expressed strong confidence in optical growth opportunities but did
not provide specific 2027 revenue guidance.

Robust financials and earnings growth, maintain Buy — Thanks to strong
demand from major CSP clients and its continuous automation and vertical
integration development, Hon Hai’s capex increased 43% YTD. Nevertheless, its
EBITDA increased 50.3% YoY to NT$369.2bn. To improve capital efficiency, Hon

Hai plans to expand consignment arrangements, optimize inventory and
receivables management, and carefully evaluate investment payback periods.
Management reiterated its long-term operating margin floor of above 3%, with
further potential upside from scale economies, automation, product mix
improvements, and greater vertical integration.
Given Hon Hai’s solid result and better operational leverage, we raise our
2026/2027/2028 EPS by 4%/5%/5%. Considering its solid execution, stable

dividend yield and attractive valuation, we reiterate our Buy rating with TP NT$400
unchanged. Our TP implies 15x of our 2027 EPS projection.

Hon Hai Precision (2317.TW)
12 November 2026 Citi Research
9
Hon Hai Precision
Company description
Established in 1974, Hon Hai has become the largest EMS player in the world
in terms of sales. Through its vertical integration model or "eCMMS" (e-
enabled Components, Modules, Moves and Services), it provides
components, modules and system assembly products to many global OEMs
in the PC, communication and consumer sectors.
Investment strategy
We rate Hon Hai shares as Buy as we believe the company’s margins will

continue to improve, backed by a renewed focus on profitability, operational
efficiency and new businesses development. We think there’s more upside to
go from current level, as the underlying drivers are not just about product
cycle, but also about fundamental transformation and new business
opportunities.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$400 is based on 15x average 2026E-27E EPS, which is
at the higher end of the historical trading range. We believe this multiple is

justified by the company’s enhanced sales growth and profit margin outlook,
as well as new endeavors into AI server, EV, digital health and robotics. Hon
Hai has been able to generate positive earnings streams, and thus we believe
a P/E valuation methodology is appropriate.
Risks
Downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price
include: 1) slower-than-expected iPhone demand; 2) intensified peer
competition; 3) weaker-than-expected execution on new business progress;

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

研 究 報 告                
旺矽(6223.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
探針卡需求強勁挹注短期營運動能強勁、CPO 設備進度如期  
 投資評價與建議 
目前探針卡廠商評價普遍修正至20~35倍，尤其以短期營運相對穩健之廠商
(如精測、旺矽)仍相對有撐，而我們觀察公司探針卡需求強勁支撐短期營運動
能強勁，並重申CPO設備驗證進度如期、客戶數持續增加，故仍看好評價仍有
望回到合理區間之中上緣。 
 重點評論分析 
4Q26營收有望雙位數季增：公司預估4Q26營收有望季增5~10%、毛利率
維持近3Q26之高檔水準，觀察探針卡BB Ratio均維持高檔水準，且擴產
計畫不變。基於公司10月營收達22.3億元創下新高、財測⼀向較為保守，
預估營收有望季增12.7%至66.6億元，毛利率61.8%，稅後EPS 21.82元。 
積極擴增MEMS產能、重申CPO客戶需求與進度如期：展望2027年：
(1)VPC和MEMS明年底有望擴至250萬針、600萬針，擴充幅度仍具有上
修空間，當前VPC主要客戶未有升級至MEMS規劃，至於MEMS主要貢獻
則來自於美系客戶伺服器CPU；(2)insertion 2客戶將於12月通知驗證結
果，1H27有望獲得量產機訂單，至於insertion 3已如期出貨工程機給3間
客戶，並預期1H27將陸續獲得量產機訂單，惟公司預估CPO設備營收顯
著貢獻時間點將落在2028年，故我們同步調整營收、獲利。 
 投資風險 
CPO進展不如預期、探針卡擴產針數下修。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況 
3Q26獲利優於預期：3Q26營收季增12.8%至59億元，連續七季創下歷史新高，毛利率61.7%，稅後EPS 19.18元，
其中營收、獲利均優於預期，推估主因為探針卡產品組合改善，加上於2Q26進行之探針卡報價調整完整貢獻3Q
營收(包含CPC漲價20%、取消VPC和MEMS 10%價格優惠)，另公司營業費用管控得宜也同步挹注獲利。 
估4Q26營收有望雙位數季增：公司預估4Q26營收有望季增5~10%、毛利率有望維持近3Q26之高檔水準，我們
推估動能包含：(1)維持VPC、MEMS月產能今年底分別擴至200萬針、350萬針之進展；(2)訂單出貨則觀察探針卡
BB Ratio均維持高檔水準，其中CPC今年前10個月維持滿載、目前BB Ratio接近1，至於MEMS、VPC則超過1，
考量公司探針卡交期已明顯延⻑至4~6個月，隱含本季探針卡營收仍有望持續提升。基於公司10月營收達22.3億
元創下新高、財測⼀向較為保守，預估本季營收有望季增12.7%至66.6億元，毛利率61.8%，稅後EPS 21.82元。 
積極擴增MEMS產能、重申CPO客戶需求與進度如期：展望2027年，我們觀察：(1)探針卡中，明年CPC月產能維
持50萬針、VPC和MEMS明年底則有望擴至250萬針、600萬針，產能擴充幅度具有上修空間，且強調2028年維持
積極擴產策略，訂單貢獻中，VPC主要客戶近年未有升級至MEMS探針卡之規劃，預期該客戶AI ASIC訂單將支撐
明年VPC需求，至於MEMS明年主要貢獻則來自於美系客戶伺服器CPU，且公司持續爭取潛在客戶AI ASIC訂單，
另規劃於2H27量產之PCB廠有望挹注探針卡成本優化；(2)CPO設備中，insertion 2如我們先前所提，於3Q26底
～4Q26初送樣至客戶端進行驗證，公司預估客戶將於12月通知驗證結果，根據目前客戶回饋正向，預期1H27有
望獲得量產機訂單，此外近期有另⼀客戶與公司接洽中(公司預估1H27獲得驗證結果)，至於insertion 3則已如期
出貨工程機給3間客戶，也將陸續交機給另3間客戶，預計1H27將陸續獲得insertion 3量產機訂單，惟考量接單至
出貨、裝機驗收之時間較⻑，公司認為CPO相關設備顯著營收貢獻時間點將落在2028年，我們則同步下修明年相
關設備之營收認列數量。綜上所述，公司預估明年營收有望年增至少50%、毛利率維持在60%以上；我們則預估
明年營收有望年增58.6%至344.6億元，較先前下修主要反映部分CPO設備遞延至2028年認列營收，毛利率63.2%，
稅後EPS 117.77元。

7788.TT 松川精密 
 趨勢不變 評價調升

[財務資訊更新，調升評價]   
   公司簡介 松川精密成立於 1974 年，主要從事繼電器之研發、製造及銷售，繼電器屬於自動控 制開關的一種，係以較小電流(訊號)連動大電流(電源)的元件。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
強力買進 348 
【前次投資建議】買進，348 
【大盤指數/股價】41040 / 178.5

➢ 高價庫存原料侵蝕毛利，3Q26 EPS 2.83 元  
松川精密 3Q26 營收 27.35 億元，QoQ+21.04%，YoY+78.84%，毛利
率 27.97%，稅後淨利 2.26 億元，QoQ-14.23%，YoY+103.23%，EPS 
2.83 元，低於投研部預期，主要歸因於毛利率季減 2.98 個百分點，包含
兩大因素：1)產品組合變化：完整認列台興營收，單季營收佔比 3.6%，
且高毛利儲能、AI 繼電器相關產品佔比下滑 1 至 2 個百分點；2)高價原
料成本影響：上半年白銀曾衝上每盎司 121.6 美元的歷史天價，公司 8~9
月出貨產品採用高價白銀原料。 
➢ 最壞情況已過，4Q26 獲利重返單季新高 
10 月營收 8.49 億元，MoM-3.19%，YoY+53.62%，達成率 32%，符
合預期，部分產品出貨受船期影響，致營收遞延認列，整體單季營收與
原先預估差異不大。毛利率方面，下半年白銀每季平均跌幅 12%~14%，
目前回落至 50 美元以下，原料平均成本已下降，10 月高價原料衝擊影
響性降低，毛利率回升，預期單季最壞情況已過，4Q26 毛利率回升至
29.11%，EPS 3.33 元，獲利重返成長。 
➢ AI 伺服器高壓直流架構，繼電器/接觸器產值增加近兩倍 
AI 伺服器從 54V 升級至±400V 或 800V 高壓直流架構，必須額外配置
高壓直流接觸器，技術門檻遠高於傳統產品，單價達到交流繼電器的 20
倍至 30 倍，投研部推測每機櫃至少需配置 12 至 28 顆不等，單機櫃產
值成長 198%。松川 225A/900V 產品已率先邁入小量出貨階段，直流接
觸器將是松川 2027 年的核心成長引擎，預估松川 2027 年 AI 伺服器繼
電器/接觸器營收成長 50%~60%，營收佔比 20.2%。 
➢ 評等調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元 
投研部預估松川精密 2026 年/2027 年 EPS 分別為 11.68 元及 15.58
元，2027 年各應用面展望樂觀，儲能及 AI 伺服器仍是主軸，雖然毛利
率較原先下修 1 個百點，但上修直流接觸器產值預估，加上費用控管優
於預期，2027 年整體獲利小幅上修。評價方面，目前松川本益比僅 11
倍，處於歷史 PE 區間 10X~30X 下緣，投資價值浮現，投研部將松川投
資評等，由買進調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元(2027F PE23X)。

個股報告

高價庫存原料侵蝕毛利，3Q26 EPS 2.83 元，低於預期 
松川精密 3Q26 營收 27.35 億元，QoQ+21.04%，YoY+78.84%，毛利率 27.97%，
稅後淨利 2.26 億元，QoQ-14.23%，YoY+103.23%，EPS 2.83 元，低於投研部預期，
主要歸因於毛利率季減 2.98 個百分點，包含兩大因素：1)產品組合變化：完整認列台
興營收，單季營收佔比 3.6%，且高毛利儲能、AI 繼電器相關產品佔比下滑 1 至 2 個
百分點；2)高價原料成本衝擊：白銀在 2026 上半年曾衝上每盎司 121.6 美元的歷史
天價，公司 8~9 月出貨產品採用高價白銀原料，對 8~9 月的單月毛利率造成短期壓抑。

### Page 3

個股報告 
民主黨勝選有利綠能產業發展，儲能剛性需求強 
根據產業統計，2026 年前三季全球儲能總併網量估計達 220 至 240 GWh，系統出貨
量約 420 GWh。受惠於第四季併網旺季，BloombergNEF 於 10 月將 2026 全年新增
裝機預估上調至 188 GW / 535 GWh，並預期 2027 年將進一步成長至 209 GW / 638 
GWh，容量年增 19.3%。太陽能方面，全球新增裝機在 2025 年創高後，2026 年受
中國電網消納限制影響，預計微幅回落至 584 至 638 GW；但海外市場逆勢成長 15%
至 313 GW。展望 2027 年，全球裝機預估將回升至 618 GW，產業呈現「光弱儲強」
趨勢，加速光儲一體化建置。 
綜合上述，全球光儲兩大核心市場皆集中於中美兩國，加上美國民主黨於 2026 年期
中選舉勝出並奪下國會主導權，綠能與清潔能源政策延續性獲得有力支撐，可望進一
步推升當地光儲建置動能。中美兩地領導廠商均為松川精密的核心客戶，受惠於美系
客戶新一代儲能櫃容量由 3.9 MWh 提升至 5 MWh，工作電流增加帶動接觸器規格與
單價雙雙看漲，預估 2027 年松川在儲能、太陽能及微電網業務將維持 10%~15%的
成長。 
AI 伺服器高壓直流架構，繼電器/接觸器產值增加近兩倍 
AI 伺服器從 54V 升級至±400V 或 800V 高壓直流架構，機櫃配電單元(PDU)面臨嚴
峻的高壓電弧安全挑戰，必須額外配置高壓直流接觸器(DC Contactor)，為在毫秒內
強制切斷(滅弧)800V 高壓直流電弧，產品內部高度仰賴磁吹滅弧設計或真空密封充氣
(氫氣／氮氣)工藝，技術門檻遠高於傳統交流繼電器(AC Relay)，單價達到交流繼電器
的 20 倍至 30 倍。在用量方面，每座機櫃 PDU 至少需配置 12 顆，主流高階規格更
需 24 顆至 28 顆，單機櫃直流接觸器產值為既有交流繼電器的 2 至 3 倍。松川
225A/900V 產品已率先邁入小量出貨階段，未來隨著高壓直流架構全面放量，直流接
觸器將正式擔綱松川 2027 年的核心成長引擎。在額外增加直流接觸器需求下，投研
部推測高壓直流架構(不含自動轉換開關 ATS)，AI 伺服器繼電器/接觸器單機櫃產值將
從 7,000 元增加至 20,900 元，YoY+198%，預估松川 2027 年 AI 伺服器繼電器/接觸
器營收成長 50%~60%，營收佔比 20.2%。 
預估松川 2027 年 EPS 15.58 元，評等調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元 
預估松川精密 2026 年營收 95.52 億元，YoY+48.49%，毛利率 28.80%，稅後淨利
9.31 億元，YoY+246.03%，EPS 11.68 元；預估 2027 年 113.14 億元，YoY+18.45%，
毛利率 30.53%，稅後淨利 12.43 億元，YoY+33.42%，EPS 15.58 元。2027 年各應
用面展望樂觀，儲能及 AI 伺服器仍是主軸，雖然毛利率較原先下修 1 個百點，但上修
直流接觸器產值預估，加上費用控管優於預期，2027 年整體獲利小幅上修。評價方
面，目前松川本益比僅 11 倍，處於歷史 PE 區間 10X~30X 下緣，投資價值浮現，投
研部將松川投資評等，由買進調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元(2027F PE23X)。

### Page 1

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.15  
잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기

3Q26 Preview 
기아는 3Q26E 매출액 31.1조원(+8.2% YoY, -6.0% QoQ), 영업이익 2.2조원
(+53.1% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 7.2%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 
물량은 좋았으나, 아쉬운 믹스와 환율 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.8% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.0만대
(+8.6%)를 기록했다. EV·HEV 확대에 따른 ASP 상승은 지속되고 있다. 전쟁으로 인
한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 재료비 부담은 전분기 대비 3,000억~4,000억원 증가할 
것으로 예상된다. EV 판매 비중은 2분기 13%에서 3분기 15%, 유럽은 35%에서 
43%로 확대되며 유럽 내 마진 희석은 불가피하다. 원화 강세에 따른 영업상 부담은 
판매보증충당부채 환입 등으로 상쇄돼 3분기 환율 영향은 제한적일 전망이다. 판매보
증충당부채 환입은 QoQ +5,000억원 수준으로 추정된다. 4분기부터 환율 효과는 부정
적일 것이며, 환율 10원 변동 시 연간 손익 민감도는 약 1,500억원 수준이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 165,000원으로 하향 
미국 조지아 공장의 텔루라이드 HEV(2.8만대)와 HMGMA의 스포티지 HEV(1.0만대) 
신규 생산으로 북미의 견조한 HEV 수요에 대응하며, 2027년 믹스 개선이 기대된다. 
유럽은 EV3·EV5로 점유율을 확대하는 가운데 유럽 내 할인율 하락과 EV 잔존가치 
회복으로 판매 여건이 개선되고 있다. 향후 IAA 규제에 따른 법인·플릿 혜택 제한은 
중국 업체의 원가 우위를 줄일 수 있을 전망이다. 
원화 약세 지속을 반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다.  
다만, 현재 주가는 12M Fwd P/E 기준 4.8배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 
수준, 저가 매수 기회라 판단된다. 현재 배당 수익률은 6% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높은 
배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

주가 추이

3Q26 매출액과 영업이익은 비현금성 비용 항목 개선으로 시장 기대치
를 상회했으나, 차량 판매 감소와 인플레이션 등으로 연간 가이던스는 
하향되었습니다. 올해 생산 차질에 따라 이연된 신차 판매를 ’27년에 
확대하며 매출/이익 회복을 가시화하는 부분에 주목 필요합니다. 
매출 성장은 금융부문이, 영업이익은 자동차부문이 기여 
동사 3Q26 매출액은 45.1조원(-3.5% 이하 YoY), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%)을 기록하며 시장 기대치를 상회했다. 파업으로 인한 생산 차질
로 3Q 판매량 92.1만대(-11.3%)에 그치며 자동차부문 매출은 큰 폭으
로 감소(33조원, -10.0%)했으나, 금융부문 매출이 취급 자산 증가로 큰 
폭으로 성장(+27.8%)했다. 반면, 영업이익은 금리 상승에 따른 금융부문 
영업비용 확대 영향과 기타부문 실적 부진 발생과 달리 자동차부문은 전
년 동기 대비 관세율 차이에 따른 비용 감소와 판매보증 충당부채 전입
액 감소 등 우호적 환율효과로 큰 폭의 증가세(+24.8%) 기록했다. 
’26년 가이던스 하향, 구조적 반등 요인은 아직 
3Q 실적 발표에서 주목할 부분은 가이던스 하향과 현금흐름이다. 연간 
판매목표를 연초 415.8만대에서 400만대로 하향했고 영업이익률 목표
는 6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 0.8%p 하향했으며, ’26년 투자계획은 
17.8조원에서 16조원으로 낮췄다. 특히, 투자계획 하향은 차량 판매 매
출 감소 대비 인센티브, 원자재 가격 상승 등으로 현금흐름이 악화된 
부분에 기인한 것으로 판단된다. 실적발표에서 전사적 원가 최적화 활
동 추진을 강조했으나, 이는 좀 더 장기적 측면에서 효과가 가시화될 
것으로 판단되며, 인플레이션 및 원화절상 상황 고려 시 구조적 반등은 
단기적으로 쉽지 않을 수 있다. 
절치부심의 2027년, 목표주가 600.000원 유지 
그러나 매크로 상황을 제외하면  ’26년 하반기 생산 차질에 따른 신차 
판매효과가 ’27년으로 이연된 것이라는 점에 주목 필요하다.   ‘북미 HEV, 유럽 
BEV’ 전략 하에서 엘란트라, 투싼 풀세인지, GV80, G80 등 고수익 HEV 차종 
중심 미국 수요 대응을 통해 Mix 개선하는 모습이 확인되고, 생산이 본격화된 
아이오닉3 유럽 판매 확대 시 중국 로컬업체들의 공세를 방어하는 모습을 
확인하는 것이 가능할 것으로 판단한다. 노후차종 중심 운영에 따른 판매 둔화 
이외에 아직 근원적 판매 경쟁력 저하로는 판단하지 않는다.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10 월 30 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 6

현대차 (005380) [한화리서치]

6 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일)

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
아직 수요의 끝은 보이지 않는다 
AI 투자에 대한 우려의 목소리도 있지만 정작 부품을 공급하고 투자하는 쪽의 목소
리는 시간이 갈수록 더 커지고 있다. 공급 부족의 시점 매 분기 뒤로 밀려나고 있기 
때문이다. 28년까지 공급부족이라고 했던 메모리업체들은 이구동성으로 30년까지 공
급부족이 지속된다고 언급하고 있다. 이러한 변화는 실적에 고스란히 반영되고 있
다. 26년 3분기 메모리 영업이익만 108.5조원으로 추정한다. 4분기에는 140조원에 
이를 것으로 전망한다. 
HBM4와 QLC는 차별화 포인트 
삼성전자는 HBM에서 입지가 좋았던 적이 없었다. 점유율, 수익성, 가동률, 제품 성
능, 고객 등 모든 항목에서 경쟁사 대비 열위에 있었다. HBM4는 모든 항목에서 입
지를 개선시킬 변수가 될 것으로 예상한다. 26년 3분기 물량은 경쟁사 수준일 것으
로 보이고 연간 물량도 유사한 수준일 전망이다. 27년에는 가격 인상과 가동률 상승
으로 수익성도 크게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. QLC는 TLC와 함께 AI 모델 진화의 
수혜를 받을 제품으로 빠르게 부상하고 있다. 메타의 Muse 영향이다. 접속자당 가
상 메모리를 NAND에 100GB를 할당하고 있기 때문이다.  
부족한 주주환원으로 투자 우선 순위에서 밀릴 듯 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 
변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, HBM 
시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 
판단하기 때문이다. 목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황
을 고려하면 적정 주가는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치
에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 
제한적일 것으로 판단하기 때문이다.

### Page 2

삼성전자 (005930)

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기 매출액은 195.3조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.9% 증가한 195.3조원이다. VD/
가전을 제외한 주요 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 증가했다. DS, MX 모두 물량보다는 
가격 효과가 더 크게 작용한 것으로 분석된다. 사업부별로는  
1) DS 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 15.4% 증가한 147.1조원이다. 메모리
는 26년 2분기 대비 16.4% 증가했다. 26년 3분기 DRAM B/G(Bit Growth)
는 +2.0%, ASP는 +20.0%이다. NAND B/G는 +5.0%, ASP는 +18.0%이다. 
HBM 물량 증가는 HBM4가 주도했고, 그 이하 제품 비중은 하락한 것으로 
추정한다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 7.1% 증가한 8조원이다. 소형
은 해외 고객 및 국내 고객의 신제품 출시 영향으로 ASP 상승, 물량 증가 
효과가 동반되었고, 대형은 게이밍 모니터 비중을 확대했다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.4% 증가한 37.7조원이
다. MX는 15.5% 증가했다. 물량은 소폭 감소했고, ASP 상승 영향을 받았다. 
네트워크 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 60% 감소했다.  
4) VD/가전 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 4.1% 감소한 13.9조원이다. VD 
매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 6.5% 감소했다. 가전은 26년 2분기 대비 1.3% 
감소했다.

IBKS RESEARCH │3 
26년 3분기 영업이익은 107.4조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 20% 증가한 107.4조원이다. DS,  
디스플레이와 MX는 2분기 대비 증가했고, VD/가전은 2분기 대비 감소했다. 사업부별
로는 
1) DS 사업부는 26년 2분기 대비 19.4% 증가한 106.5조원이다. 2분기 대비 영
업이익률은 소폭 상승했다. 메모리 영업이익은 2분기 대비 19.0% 증가했고, 
비메모리는 영업적자를 유지했다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 54.5% 증가한 1.1조원이다. 
중소형 신제품 효과로 수익성이 개선된 영향이다. 대형도 믹스 개선으로 수
익성이 개선되었다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자폭이 감소했다. 물량
은 줄었지만 High End 비중 상승으로 ASP는 개선되었고, 원가 비중이 낮아
진 영향이다. 
4) VD/가전 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자 규모가 커졌다.

### Page 6

삼성전자 (005930)

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 4분기 매출액은 226.5조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 16.0% 증가한 226.5조원으로 예상
한다. DS가 증가하고 나머지는 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS는 메모리가 성장을 주도할 것으로 기대한다. 가격과 물량 모두 3분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 전망한다. Context Server에 대한 수요가 증가하고, Personal Agent의 가상메
모리 설정으로 인해 NAND 수요가 증가하면서 QLC에 대한 수요 증가 속도가 빨라질 
전망이다. 이로 인해 NAND Bit Growth는 경쟁사와 차별화된 수준일 것으로 예상한
다.  
HBM 물량도 꾸준히 증가할 것으로 기대한다. 3분기에도 HBM4를 중심으로 물량이 
크게 증가했다. HBM4는 SK하이닉스와 비슷한 물량일 것으로 예상한다. 아직 전체 
물량은 SK하이닉스가 더 많은 상황이고, HBM 내 HBM4 비중은 업계 최고 수준인 것
으로 판단한다. 26년까지는 HBM이 수익성에는 마이너스 요인이지만, 27년에는 
DRAM 수준의 영업이익이 가능할 것으로 기대한다.  
MX는 비수기 저가 라인 물량 조정으로 전체 물량은 감소하지만, 제품믹스는 개선되
어 판가는 3분기 대비 상승할 것으로 예상한다.

26년 4분기 영업이익은 138.2조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 28.7% 증가한 138.2조원으로 예
상한다. DS 사업부를 제외하고 모두 3분기 대비 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS 사업부 영업이익은 3분기 대비 30% 증가한 138.5조원으로 전망한다. DRAM, 
NAND 가격이 10% 초중반 수준으로 상승하고, 물량은 10% 중반 이상 증가하면서 3
분기 대비 영업이익률 개선을 기대하기 때문이다. HBM 물량 증가가 이익 개선에 미
치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상한다.  
디스플레이, MX는 물량 부진으로 3분기 대비 영업이익은 부진할 것으로 전망한다.

DS가 성장 주도 
DS만 보자

### Page 7

김운호  6915-5656

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
Check Point 
1. 모자란 듯한 주주환원 
26년 3분기 실적 발표에서 밝힌 주주환원 정책은 지난 8월의 내용과 다르지 않다. 3
분기 배당 30조원(주당 4,601원)을 확정한 정도이다. 잔여 배당 재원이 55~75조원으
로 구체화했고, 현금 배당과 자사주 매입을 병행하겠다고 밝혔다.  
자사주 매입 시 우선주 매입도 고려 대상이라고 언급한 것은 고무적이지만 27년부터 
새롭게 시작되는 주주환원 정책을 결정하기 위한 이사회 개최는 27년 1월에 열릴 것
으로 예고하였기에 연내 추가적인 주주환원 발표는 없을 것으로 판단한다.  
2. HBM4로 HBM 시장에서 입지 강화 
HBM4를 통해서 자신감을 회복하고 있는 것으로 판단한다. 양산 시점은 경쟁사 대비 
빨랐고, 3분기 물량은 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준인 것으로 추정한다. HBM3e가 부진했기 
때문에 전체 생산량에서 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 가장 높은 수준일 것으로 추정한다.  
26년 전체 물량도 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준이 될 것으로 전망한다. 삼성전자는 HBM 시
장 점유율을 DRAM 수준으로 맞추겠다는 목표를 3분기에 달성한 것으로 밝혔다.  
물량이 증가하면서 가동률도 빠르게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. 아직은 DRAM과 영업
이익률에서 큰 차이가 있지만 27년에는 인상된 가격, 높아진 수율, 가동률 개선으로 
DRAM과 영업이익률 차이를 좁힐 수 있을 것으로 기대한다.  
3. QLC NAND 시장 확대 
AI 컴퓨팅 확산은 NAND에게도 성장 기회이다. AI 모델들의 Context Window가 길어
져 KV Cache 메모리에 대한 수요가 커지면서 High Endurance TLC 수요가 급증하고 
있다. DRAM을 대체할 수준의 NAND가 필요하게 되었기 때문이다.  
이 뿐만 아니라 고용량 NAND에 대한 수요도 Personal Agent의 출현으로 급속히 증
가할 것으로 기대한다. 여기에는 QLC NAND가 주로 사용된다. 메타의 Muse는 접속
자 1인에 대해서 DRAM 8GB, NAND 100GB의 가상 메모리를 설정하고 운영한다.  
Agent AI의 확산은 스토리지에 대한 수요도 급격히 확대할 것으로 기대하고, 삼성전
자에게도 기회가 될 것으로 기대한다.  
26년 4분기 NAND Bit Growth는 삼성전자가 가장 높을 것으로 예상한다.

### Page 8

삼성전자 (005930)

8 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 350,000원으로 하향 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 
1) 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 
2) 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, 
3) HBM 시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 
4) 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 판단하기 때문이다. 
목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황을 고려하면 적정 주가
는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사
와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 제한적일 것으로 판단하기 
때문이다.

Document 298: Chipping in_ how chips have become a huge driver of the dollar (2026-11-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chipping in_ how chips have become a huge driver of the dollar (2026-11-16).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Chipping in: how chips have  
become a huge driver of the dollar

1 
Authors 
Mallika Sachdeva 
Strategist

November 16, 2026 
What do chips have to do with a new era of dollar dominance? As we discuss in 
this paper – a great deal. Chips are not only critical to the future of AI but have 
recently emerged as a huge driver of global trade and capital flows. 
It is well known that the US is dominant in upstream chip technology and 
downstream compute. This note delves deeper into the workings of the middle of 
the chain where flows have been reinforcing the dollar’s global role. The 
manufacturing of chips largely takes place in Asia where chip-related trade 
surpluses have ballooned. As we show, these surpluses are finding their way 
back to the US via investments in US capital markets. 
Asian chip surpluses are not being recycled into US Treasuries like petrodollar 
surpluses were, but into US equities. Foreign inflows into US equities have 
crossed USD1tn a year and become the mainstay of US deficit funding. The 
biggest source of increase over the past year has come from Asia. 
Equity flows have not just been supporting the US dollar but have provided 
financing to the companies driving the AI buildout and chips boom itself. This 
introduces forms of circularity and potential instability to global flows. Moreover, 
equities are a much riskier asset class than bonds – US stocks are three times as 
volatile as Treasuries, with tech stocks up to ten times as much. Growing dollar 
dependence on inherently riskier capital should spill into higher US dollar 
volatility over time. 
The rise of chips as a driver of dollar dominance can be seen as a symptom of 
change in the ultimate source of US power - from geopolitical to technological 
leadership. This power is increasingly concentrated in the private sector with 
dollar supportive flows intermediated via private balance sheets. 
Understanding this new framework and its risks is even more important given 
rapid shifts in news flow around AI, from the US political climate, high-stakes 
capital market raisings, potential tightening in export controls, and the highly 
consequential technology race with China.

### Page 2

Deutsche Bank AG Page 2

The rise of the chip dollar 
For the first time in modern history, global trade in semiconductors now 
exceeds trade in oil and gas (Figure 1) based on the four quarters of global 
exports till Q2-2026. More relevant from a currency perspective, is that chip 
surpluses in Asia ex-China have caught up to petroleum-related surpluses in the 
Gulf (Figure 2).  Why does this matter and what does this reflect?

Figure 1:  Global trade in chips has overtaken trade in 
oil & gas for the first time in history

Figure 2:  Asia (ex-China)’s chip surpluses are now as 
large as the Gulf’s petroleum-related surpluses

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, ITC Trade Map; Note: Oil & Gas are HS Codes 2709, 
2710, 2711.; Semiconductors is HS 8542

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, ITC Trade Map; Note: Oil & Gas are HS 2709, 2710, 
2711.; Semiconductors is HS 8542; Asia ex-China is Japan, Korea, Taiwan & ASEAN

We argue that the petrodollar is – rather quickly - being superseded by the chip-
dollar. This is both literal in terms of global surplus accumulation, but also 
archetypal. In other words, the petrodollar and chip-dollar are archetypes for two 
different states of the world, and for alternative foundations of dollar dominance.  
The petrodollar regime can be understood as one where US geopolitical 
leadership – over global security, international rules, and free trade – motivated 
global savings to be reinvested in the US sovereign via Treasury holdings. This 
cheapened funding for the US government which in turn supported the financing 
of US public goods provision.  
The emerging chip-dollar regime is one where US technology leadership is 
motivating global savings to be reinvested in US companies – providing funding 
for the US corporate sector to make enormous investments in new technology 
such as AI.  
The transition from the petrodollar to the chip-dollar is therefore a 
manifestation of change in the US’ leadership style - from geopolitical to 
technology leadership. This is not only changing the nature of global imbalances 
and capital flow recycling but is reshaping the foundations of dollar dominance.  
Revisiting the petrodollar regime  
After the 1971 Nixon shock, when the dollar’s convertibility to gold was 
terminated, we moved into a fully fiat currency world. The US dollar since then 
has been backed only by the fiscal credibility of the US government and the 
willingness of the world to finance US deficits by buying US assets.  
16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 3

Deutsche Bank AG Page 3

The 1974 petrodollar deal provided a crucial pillar of support for the dollar in its 
fiat infancy and sowed the seeds for a post-Bretton Woods regime in which US 
geopolitical leadership supported dollar dominance.  In 1974, in exchange for US 
security and military guarantees, Saudi Arabia agreed to price and invoice oil sales 
in USD. These USD denominated oil surpluses were then reinvested in US 
Treasuries. This was soon replicated across the Gulf. The 1990s and 2000s saw 
huge growth in commodity and manufacturing surpluses around the world. These 
were arguably supported by the US’s global backstop for trade and security – with 
surpluses saved in USD and recycled into US Treasuries via public balance sheets, 
in what can be considered a petrodollar-plus regime.  
We wrote earlier in the year how the Iran war could undermine the critical 
foundations of the petrodollar system by challenging the US security umbrella 
and shifting perceptions around US geopolitical leadership. We maintain that 
these changes are underway. But what has become apparent since then is that 
something else has been rising to take the petrodollar’s place in support of the 
dollar.  
The chip dollar is working differently  
While petrodollar surpluses were recycled into US Treasuries, chips surpluses 
are being recycled very differently.  
We focus on the chip surpluses being built in Asia ex-China. We note that China 
runs a very large semiconductor deficit, despite having the world’s largest overall 
trade surplus - something we will return to later. Asia’s chip surpluses are thus 
primarily hosted in Korea and Taiwan which are large semiconductor 
manufacturers, to a lesser extent in Japan which produces key materials, and 
ASEAN-10 which plays a role in assembly, packaging and testing.  
To explore how chip dollar surpluses are being recycled, we zoom in on South 
Korea – the epitome of the boom in chips trade.  
Korea’s semiconductor exports have grown over 200% YoY this year, with Korean 
chipmakers dominating in the manufacture of memory chips critical to AI 
acceleration. As Figure 3 shows, Korea’s semiconductor trade surplus has 
ballooned to close to USD250bn with 100% of Korea’s overall trade surplus now 
on account of chips alone.  
What is striking is that Korea’s chip surplus is being recycled abroad entirely via 
equities. Foreign sales of Korean equities have accounted for half the capital 
outflows in the past year, while Korean purchases of overseas equities account 
for the other half (Figure 3). Korean investments in overseas equities have been 
five times more than Korean purchases of overseas bonds in the last two years. In 
short, chip surpluses are being saved in global equities, not debt. 
The primary destination of Korea’s overseas equity purchases has been the US. 
While the US accounts for around 50% of global equity market capitalization, we 
note it is 70% of Korea’s overseas equity holdings (IMF CPIS). The US bias is even 
more exaggerated for Korean retail investors – a group empowered by access to 
financial technology. Korean households have 90% of their equity exposure in the 
US (Figure 4). It appears that North Asian capital is flowing back to the very US 
technology companies that are driving the chip surpluses themselves.

16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 4

Deutsche Bank AG Page 4

Figure 3:  Asian chip surpluses are being recycled into 
US equities – not US Treasuries like petrodollars were

Figure 4: 70% of Korea’s equity exposure abroad is to 
the US, and that figure is more than 90% for households

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, CEIC; Note: The semiconductor trade balance here is 
measured using Korea’s Customs Service classification for Semiconductors and 
equipment for semiconductor manufacturing

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics; Note: Data based on IMF’s 
Coordinated Portfolio Investment Survey as of end-2025

The reach for US equities by Asian retail is not just happening in Korea. In Japan, 
there have been significant allocations to US focused ETFs, encouraged by the 
NISA scheme. And as we have argued in our earlier paper, the US’s embrace of 
asset tokenization and the upcoming shift by US exchanges to 23/5 trading could 
further embolden and enable retail investors to access US equities. We could thus 
just be at the beginning of this new trend. Countries in North Asia like Korea and 
Japan not only have large savings pools, but also open capital accounts.  
This shift has shown up dramatically on the US capital account. Foreign 
purchases of US equities have exceeded USD1tn over the past year (Figure 5). 
Interestingly, most of the increase in purchases has come from Asia (Figure 6). 
Notably, foreign purchases of US equities are now more than double US Treasury 
allocations, which appear in relative decline. Even foreign purchases of US 
corporate bonds now exceed US Treasuries. This is notable considering the ramp 
up in hyper scaler issuance.

Figure 5: Foreign flows into US stocks now exceed 
flows into US Treasuries by a significant margin

Figure 6: Most of the increase in US equity purchases 
over the past year has come from Asia

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics (TICS data)

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics (TICS data)

16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 5

Deutsche Bank AG Page 5

Chips are the oil of the new economy  
The power of having oil priced and paid for in dollars was not just the recycling 
of petrodollar surpluses, but the value chain impact of having a key upstream 
resource dollarized. Oil is a critical input to almost every industrial process via 
petrochemicals – it is found in everything from plastic to paints, tires to 
cosmetics. It also plays a role in moving traded goods around the world as the 
transport fuel for ships. We would argue that having oil priced in dollars played a 
role in dollarizing global trade as companies looked to match the currency of 
downstream receivables to the upstream costs of oil. This is consistent with the 
Dominant Currency Paradigm (Gopinath et al, 2020).  
Chips arguably play a similar role in the digital and consumer economy as oil has 
played in the industrial economy. Chips are inputs to everything from consumer 
electronics, to automobiles, computers, communication devices, and most 
recently the bedrock of modern AI systems.  
Indeed, chips make up the lion’s share of spending in the AI buildout. Analysis by 
Epoch AI found that servers represented about 60% of the annualized cost of an 
indicative 1GW data centre in the US (Figure 7). According to the Semiconductor 
Industry Association (SIA), chips represent 95% of an AI server rack's value. In 
simple terms therefore, we can say chips make up over 50% of total capital 
expenditures for an AI data centre. Bloomberg Intelligence estimates hyper-
scaler capital expenditure commitments of close to USD8tn out to 2031 (Figure 
8), which would suggest a further USD4tn of chips demand from AI alone.

Figure 7: More than half the cost of the AI data centre 
build out is going towards chips

Figure 8:  Capital expenditure in the AI buildout may 
still have a long way to go

Source: Deutsche Bank Research

To be sure, the market will remain sensitive to shifts in news flow and risks that 
the AI data centre buildout might slow whether due to the changing political 
climate in the US, calls for growing public oversight and safety monitoring of the 
industry, or a renewed focus on export controls.  
There are also open questions as to whether the boom in chips demand will be 
“one-off” as capacity is built or will be ongoing like with oil. Oil is a huge source of 
ongoing dollar flows primarily because of how consistent and trade-intensive it 
16 November 2026
 FX Special Report

### Page 6

Deutsche Bank AG Page 6

is: 100 million barrels are consumed daily with 70% of them being traded. We note 
that the replacement rate of chips in an average data centre is estimated at 3-7 
years, which suggests there could be ongoing demand even once the projected 
capex buildout reaches its zenith. But it remains to be seen whether the chips 
dollar can generate the type of enduring and stable recycling that the petrodollar 
has generated. 
Staying with the comparison between the petrodollar and chip-dollar, arguably it 
was US military presence in the world’s largest oil producing region in the 1970s 
– the Gulf - that gave the US control over the pricing of oil. What is it that gives 
the US control over the pricing and trade in chips? 
US control over chips: technology, economic rents, and supply chains   
The integrated circuit was invented in the US in 1958 with the microchip industry 
flourishing in California to become the well-known Silicon Valley. It is hard to 
overstate the continued dominance of the US in chips since then. Work by Sean 
Kenji Starrs of Kings College London maps the present-day dominance in 
compelling terms1. 
While most chip manufacturing happens in Asia – which is where the trade 
surpluses discussed above are being accumulated – most of the economic rents 
to chip-making accrues to US companies. Around 50% of the value in the 
semiconductor supply chain is in the design process where US companies control 
87% of the revenue (Starrs). The US has a near monopoly on the electronic design 
automation (EDA) software necessary for design, and controls around 60% of the 
revenue in semiconductor manufacturing equipment without which chips cannot 
be made. Materials, manufacturing, packaging and testing of chips happen 
outside the US, but these are worth only around 30% of the estimated value of 
the industry. Indeed, Starrs finds that 70% of the profits from the semiconductor 
industry flow to US firms (Figure 9).  
Furthermore, we note that all key countries involved in the supply chain of 
advanced chips remain US aligned. Japan plays an important role in materials, 
while Taiwan and Korea dominate in production (foundries). Importantly each of 
these North Asian players leans geopolitically towards the US over China. The US 
has arguably been less vocal about withdrawing economic support for security in 
North Asia than with NATO. One can thus posit the existence of a US-Asia chip-
dollar bloc. We note that the Netherlands has a monopoly on EUV lithography 
machines required to make the most advanced chips, but production of these 
machines in turn have a dependency on a US optical equipment firm helping 
grant the US crucial leverage even here.  
The potent combination of US control over key upstream technologies and 
intellectual property, alongside a supply chain located in US allies has given the 
US enormous control over chips. In recent years, the US has actively exercised 
this power via export controls to limit China’s access to US advanced chips and 
technologies. This has been the subject of books from Chip War by Chris Miller to 
Chokepoints by Edward Fishman. The importance of ensuring the integrity of US 
export controls was made apparent in recent comments by US Trade 
Representative Greer who noted that the US wants the “crown jewels” of the 
semiconductor industry to remain in the hands of the US or its allies. The US

Integration.” In Chapter 8 in Globalization in a Turbulent Era

government has arguably had significant influence over the private sector sale of 
US chips technology and may double down on this after recent events at Nvidia 
to limit both direct access to US chips and the leasing of global data centers built 
with US chips to non-US allies (CNBC).

Where does China fit in with all this?  
This note has looked at key changes in the industrial drivers of global imbalances 
(from fossil fuels to chips) and capital flows to the US (from debt to equities). 
Where does China – with the world’s biggest trade surplus – fit in?  
China now runs an over USD2 trillion manufactured goods surplus per year but 
continues to run a persistent semiconductor deficit (Figure 10). While US export 
controls limit access to the most advanced computing chips, China continues to 
import large volumes of memory and logic chips. Restrictions on access to 
advanced manufacturing equipment, particularly EUV lithography, have 
constrained China’s advances in domestic chip-making at the technological 
frontier, although greater self-sufficiency remains a major strategic priority. We 
note that the other drag on China’s trade position is commodities, mostly fossil 
fuels. Notably therefore, the remaining holes in China’s trade surplus are 
dominated by chip-dollar and petrodollar imports. In a forthcoming paper, we will 
look at China’s gains in renewable energy, new energy exports, and electrification, 
examining how these could emerge as long-term sources of power in both the AI 
race, and in increasing China’s independence from the dollar.  
The recycling of China’s manufactured goods surplus remains the topic of 
considerable debate. China’s surplus is in many ways the mirror of the US deficit, 
albeit with growing contribution from Europe, and increasingly intermediated 
through the Global South. There remains a school of thought that these Chinese 
savings find their way to the US through state banks and custodian balance 
sheets. But we note important points of difference with the chip dollar recycling 
we have discussed.  Nearly 50% of the net international assets held by Korea, 
16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 8

Deutsche Bank AG Page 8

Japan and Taiwan are in portfolio investments – namely equities and bonds. For 
China, this number is less than 20%. This is consistent with China’s capital account 
restrictions on private capital, as well as a growing geopolitical preference to limit 
exposure to US capital markets. Instead, China holds a much greater share of 
direct and other assets – reflecting growth in Chinese state-directed lending and 
acquisition of hard assets particularly in the Global South. On public reserve 
balance sheets, we note a growing preference for gold over dollar assets. Finally, 
we note a shift towards conversion of China’s surpluses, showing up in a stronger 
RMB and lower domestic rates.  
Bringing it together 
In the age of AI, it is well known that the US is dominant in both upstream chip 
technology as well as downstream computing. The US designs and controls 
supply chains for the world’s most advanced chips and is home to 10 times as 
many data centers as any other country (Stanford HAI). Both are sources of US 
power. This note has delved deeper into the workings of the middle of the chain 
where new flows are reinforcing the dollar’s global role in trade and capital flows. 
The manufacturing of high-end chips takes place in Asia ex-China and generates 
large trade surpluses there. This money is finding its way back to the US via 
Asian investments in US equities and corporate bonds. These capital flows are 
both funding the US trade deficit and providing financing to US companies that 
are driving the AI boom. Chips are thus ensuring the continued dominance of 
the dollar in global trade, payments, capital markets, and private sector savings, 
as well as supporting the US AI buildout seen as crucial to the future.  
There is an element of circularity to be conscious of: equity flows and financing 
for US AI related stocks are being funded out of Asian chip-related surpluses 
which are themselves being boosted by demand from US AI companies. This 
could introduce instability in global flows should demand be reassessed. For 
instance, if consumer demand for compute does not keep up with costly 
investments in supply, this would not just reduce earnings estimates for AI 
companies, but ultimately chip demand. One can see how this would challenge 
foreign equity financing for the US from both a push and pull perspective.  
Separately, as we noted in our last paper, proliferation of Chinese open-source 
models could also undermine the profitability of US companies and the 
attractiveness of US markets to global capital.  
The fact that the US dollar has become more dependent on foreign equity 
inflows than US Treasuries should make it inherently more volatile and exposed. 
Equities by nature are a riskier asset class than bonds. Recent realized volatility in 
S&P500 has been 3 times as much as in a US 10Y Treasuries. NASDAQ volatility is 
double that of the S&P and the Philly Semiconductor Index more than triple.  That 
US deficit financing is now dependent on capital flows into a more volatile asset 
class should spill over into currency volatility in time.  
In sum, US dollar dominance is now dependent on US technology leadership 
driven by private companies, and the flows underpinning the dollar are 
increasingly being intermediated on private sector balance sheets. A dollar driven 
by chips, AI and private capital connections should be inherently riskier.

US short-dated real interest rates are pushing back to their
highest levels since 2023 as markets get the message that the
Fed is ready to apply the brakes. These high real rates are
sucking funds out of asset classes like precious metals and are
triggering a broader fallout across emerging market currencies.
There is no reason to ﬁght this trend near term

Chris Turner, Francesco Pesole and Frantisek Taborsky
Higher US real rates are sucking funds into the dollar
USD: Two-year real swap rates are now
2.4%
The market has got the message. We understand the Fed needs to
take rates higher to get inﬂation back to target. And we are now
seeing US two-year real rates (swap rates less the zero-coupon
inﬂation swap) pushing above 2.40%. The last time rates were at
this level was in 2023. Higher real rates are especially negative for
zero-yielding precious metals and one of the reasons why gold and
silver and linked currencies like the South African rand and Thai
baht are under pressure.

On the Fed, we have seen further comments from Chris Waller
alongside the release of the October FOMC minutes over the last 24
hours. Waller has now turned ultra-hawkish and is pushing for
more than a reversal of the 75bp 'insurance' rate cuts seen last
year. However, he may be out on his own here. According to the
FOMC minutes, only a 'couple' of Fed voters favoured a faster pace
of tightening at the October meeting and the market now looks
quite settled on pricing 25bp hikes in December, January and next

summer. When could these views be challenged? Perhaps not until
energy-softened November CPI data is released on 10 December.
This all seems a very positive story for the dollar then. There has
also been speculation that foreign interest in Anthropic's
blockbuster IPO has seen some demand for the dollar this week. If
that is the case, that dollar demand could wane into tomorrow
once IPO allocations are settled. Yet there are good reasons for the
dollar to be at current levels, and DXY advancing to the 104.60 area

remains our bias for now.

day, while low initial jobless claims should point to a solid US jobs
market.

The South African Reserve Bank meets to set interest rates today.

MXN: Lack of rate protection comes
home to roost

parts of the emerging market complex succumbed to the strong
dollar. We heavily discussed the risk of peso underperformance in
our 2027 Latam FX Outlook, given that Banxico had cut the policy
rate spread protection over the Fed to very slim levels. We see no
reason for USD/MXN to turn lower until the broader dollar trend
turns. And, ultimately, that may not be until the second quarter of
next year, when US inﬂation is substantially falling.

2026 was the year of strong earnings. Global equity returns were unusually n
broad-based across regions, supported primarily by robust earnings growth — 
valuations have moderated. 
Earnings growth is poised to slow. As we enter 2027, earnings growth is on n
course to slow sharply across all major regions, growth in AI capex spending is 
likely to peak and the cost of capital remains elevated ... 
... But equities have de-rated sharply; with more attractive valuations, n
positive but slower nominal economic and earnings growth, and lower bond 
yields, inflation and oil prices, we expect equities to continue to rise, but 
with high volatility. 
We focus on Growth balanced with selective Defensives and beneficiaries of n
lower bond yields. 
Attractive opportunities are emerging in long-duration Growth companies, n
which should become more valuable as growth rates slow, particularly US 
Hyperscalers, which continue to enjoy strong (although slower) earnings growth 
and balance sheets. 
We balance this with selective defensive areas that offer reasonable valuations n
and areas that benefit from potential lower bond yields and oil prices. 
While forecast returns are highest in Asia, we focus on risk-adjusted returns and n
are overweight the US and Japan, neutral in Asia and EM, and underweight 
Europe, reflecting relative growth, profitability and valuation opportunities. 
Risks are becoming more two-sided. Bonds, oil prices and AI capex shifts could n
all increase volatility. We recommend diversification across Growth and 
selective Defensive strategies.

### Page 2

Value in Defensive Growth as earnings slow

Our theme for 2026 focused on a broadening equity bull market, with greater 
participation across regions, sectors and themes. Since then, the equity bull market has 
continued, and has widened out across the major regions despite rising oil prices and 
bond yields. The main driver has been strong corporate earnings growth across all 
regions. As we look to 2027, profit and AI capex growth look poised to slow in most 
regions. Nevertheless, recent price action has reflected this slowdown and equity 
valuations have come down substantially, particularly in the Technology sector. 
With economic activity and earnings on course to grow in 2027, albeit at a slower pace 
in nominal terms, and given the prospect of moderating inflation, bond yields and oil 
prices, we expect the equity bull market to continue. The range of potential outcomes 
has widened and, given stock concentration in the US and Asia, is particularly 
dependent on AI capex progress. Our recommended weightings reflect our 
assessment of risk-adjusted returns. We focus on: 
Growth — as earnings growth rates slow and margins moderate, investors are n
likely to reward these companies more highly, particularly as valuations become 
more attractive. AI-related companies have de-rated sharply, reflecting anxiety 
about peak AI capex spend and the possibility of slower returns. This is leaving 
attractive entry points, particularly in large capex Hyperscalers, which have strong 
balance sheets and diversified income streams. Significant upside potential has also 
opened up in more cyclical growth areas in memory and chips, although this comes 
with much higher risks and likely volatility.  
On a risk-adjusted basis, we are overweight the US and Japan, neutral Asia and n
EM, and underweight Europe.  
2026 — the year of earnings 
Key developments in 2026 form the foundations for a shift in opportunities in 2027. 
We see three standout features of global equity markets in 2026. 
Diversification has worked. Equity returns have been reasonable and very 1.
similar across all regions — reversing the trend between 2009 and 2025 when 
equity returns were consistently dominated by US outperformance. 
Strong earnings growth has been the principal driver of equity returns. 2.
All equity markets have de-rated, experiencing lower valuations. 3.
1. Diversified returns 
Overall, the past 12 months have seen broad-based returns across the regions. As 
Exhibit 1 shows, most equity markets have generated very similar total returns (the 
orange diamonds). This reverses the trend that had dominated post financial crisis 
through to 2025, when the US consistently outperformed other regions, benefiting from 
a combination of strong earnings growth and re-rating (as most of the businesses at the 
time were capital-light and high ROE).

2. Earnings have been the main driver of returns 
Earnings revisions have also been strong across the board (Exhibit 2). There are broadly 
three key drivers of this strength. First, Technology earnings have continued to grow 
robustly; second, higher oil prices have boosted earnings across the commodity 
sector; and third, the spill-over effects of the Technology capex boom have 
generally boosted earnings in HALO (heavy asset, low obsolescence) industries, 
such as Industrials. These factors have also contributed to an unusual pattern of 
earnings upgrades for 2026, 2027. So far, despite the recent equity sell-off, earnings 
revisions have also increased for 2028.

While this has been true in every region, the strongest upgrades have come in EM, largely 
reflecting huge upgrades in the Semiconductor space. This matters, because outsized 
gains in Tech Hardware stocks have pushed EM index concentration to record highs — 
Korea and Taiwan now account for about half of the MSCI EM, with Info Tech at >40%

3. Equity markets have de-rated 
Equity valuations have fallen across the board. While the US equity market remains the 
most expensive, it has moderated from close to its 100th percentile over 20 years to a 
P/E of 18.5x.  Asian markets and EM are now trading at P/E ratios well below their 
historical averages. Overall, P/E ratios are around their average over the past 35 years, 
and a similar pattern is true of PEG ratios (adjusting valuations for expected growth) 
(Exhibit 3). Of course, much of the fall in valuations, particularly in the 
Tech-concentrated US and North Asia equity markets, has been the principal driver of 
this de-rating.

The key drivers for 2027 
Equity markets typically rise so long as nominal GDP is growing, valuations are not 
excessive, and interest rates are not rising sharply.  Nevertheless, the momentum of 
growth matters. The shift lower in equity markets in recent weeks — centred around AI 
concerns — has rapidly priced in the prospect of weaker growth rates, and the sharp 
sector and factor rotations within equities have pushed up the valuation of Defensive 
and Value parts of the market. From here, we expect equities to generate positive 
returns (Exhibit 4), albeit with higher volatility than in recent years. We expect investors 
to seek companies that can sustain growth as it becomes more scarce.

Theme 1: Earnings growth is on course to slow 
Our economists expect a slowing in nominal GDP growth rates in 2027 as the AI 
investment boom peaks. At the same time, nearly all markets are likely to see their 
earnings growth rates halve at least (Exhibit 5): in the US, slowing from 36% to 11%, 
Europe from just around 18% to 7%, Japan from 24% to 9% and Asia from close to

Why should earnings growth slow down? 
(a) Hyperscaler capex growth should slow, which should result in decelerating 
earnings growth (Exhibit 6) for much of the AI infrastructure complex and weaken the 
support for some of the ‘picks and shovels’ of the AI-related capex boom, including 
Industrials. Consensus estimates show a slowdown in IT earnings growth from close to 
90% in 2026 to 45% in 2027 and expect Communication Services to slow from growth of 
34% in 2026 to -5% in 2027. (b) Furthermore, depreciation charges should rise 
alongside other costs, such as interest charges. Together, headwinds to growth should 
boost the attractiveness of companies that can sustain higher growth and stable 
margins. This would be relatively good for the US Hyperscalers (whose valuations have 
fallen), but raises more risks for the chip names and Industrial Cyclicals, which have 
enjoyed the benefit of accelerating AI-related capex.

Theme 2: Cost of capital is higher 
Why is the cost of capital higher? 
First, higher inflation has pushed up policy rates. Our rates strategists expect to see a 1.
modest tightening cycle, but slow progress towards cutting interest rates in 2027. 
Second, we have argued that this has reflected the combined impact of a surge in 2.
private sector demand for capital and rising government borrowing. Our bond 
strategists do see some relief in longer-term rates, which should be more supportive 
for equities (Exhibit 8) but our credit strategists expect credit spreads to widen from 
current levels.

Investment Themes 
Broadly, we would focus on three areas within equities: Growth, selective Defensives and 
beneficiaries of lower oil prices and bond yields. The expressions that best fit these 
themes vary somewhat across regions and there is some overlap, particularly between 
defensive areas and bond beneficiaries. Exhibit 9 shows a high level summary. More 
details can be found in the specific regional outlook reports.

### Page 9

Growth 
1) Growth versus Value 
For most of the period after the financial crisis through to the end of Covid-19, equity 
markets were dominated by Growth companies. A combination of surging technology 
profitability and ultra-low interest rates favoured Growth. At the same time, 
disinflationary pressures coupled with low energy prices and poor Banks returns 
(particularly in Europe), became headwinds for Value. Since 2022, however, the 
patterns have changed. Growth has still done well, but rising inflation and interest rates, 
coupled with higher energy prices and a capex boom, have strengthened the 
performance of many Value sectors. In all regions Value has sharply outperformed 
Growth in recent months (Exhibit 10).

We favour selective Defensives rather than a broad defensive shift.  We focus on 
defensive sectors that would also benefit from lower bond yields. Utilities and Telecoms 
in Europe, Healthcare and companies consistently buying back stock in the US, and Asian 
power companies are examples. Quality should benefit from slower growth and tighter 
financial conditions, but valuation discipline remains important. High dispersion should 
create a better environment for stock-picking, with a focus on stable earnings, structural

Healthcare, Power and Buyback Aristocrats; Asia—Shareholder-return strategies, 
secure dividends, strong balance sheets and companies with high and stable 
margins. In Europe, we would include Telecoms and Utilities. 
Investment focus: Selective beneficiaries of lower oil prices and bond 
yields 
Some normalisation in the Middle East and a recovery in Persian Gulf supply should 
reduce geopolitical risk premia and bring down commodity prices, including European 
gas prices. Our commodities team forecast that Brent will fall from around $100/bbl at 
end-2026 to $78/bbl at end-2027, turning the energy-price impulse from a 0.4pp drag 
on global growth into a modest boost in 2027. Combined with fading US tariff effects 
and easing AI-related supply pressures, this should lower inflation and help central 
banks bring it back towards target. The Fed and ECB should move towards rate cuts in 
late 2027, supporting modestly lower bond yields. Our rates forecasts remain more 
dovish than market pricing.  
Investment implications. Lower inflation and bond yields should support long-duration 
Growth and rate-sensitive sectors. Falling energy costs should strengthen consumers’

Overweight the US  
While the US equity market outperformed consistently over the 15 years post the 
financial crisis, its relative performance has faded since 2025. Over the course of 2026, 
the US equity market has seen more of a valuation squeeze than other markets; its ROE 
has increased relative to the rest of the world, while its relative P/B has contracted 
(Exhibit 17). Overall, we see US equity valuations as attractive versus history and also 
compared with other markets when adjusted for growth and likely volatility. We are 
underweight Europe; while lower oil prices would provide an important boost for 
European economies, it would also have a negative impact on earnings growth. 
Valuations have not fallen as much as in other regions, and the risks are higher given 
government debt issues and the heavy political calendar.

The devaluation of emerging market equities has been particularly notable through 
2026 (Exhibit 18). Driven by the AI investment boom, outsized gains in Tech Hardware 
stocks have pushed EM index concentration to record highs — Korea and Taiwan now 
account for about half of the MSCI EM, with Info Tech at >40% and the top 3 stocks (the 
‘AI trio’ of TSMC, Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix) at ~30%. Although the recent 
sell-off leaves room for a potential strong recovery, the risks remain higher than for the 
Hyperscalers. For MXAPJ (incl. Korea/Taiwan), we forecast a 17% USD price return and 
20% total return, supported by 26%/13% EPS growth in 2027/28 and a 9.3x target P/E, 
but Ex-Korea/Taiwan, we forecast 12% USD price return and 15% total return, and

What are the risks? 
We see four in particular: bond volatility, oil volatility, weaker AI capex, and momentum 
concentration — each of which could generate both downside as well as upside risks for 
investors.  
1. The bond market 
The rise in bond yields through 2026 has, so far, largely been offset in equity markets by 
strong earnings growth. That said, it has contributed to lower P/E ratios, and the 
underperformance of sectors that are most sensitive to interest rates, such as Real 
Estate and Defensives, while contributing to the relative strength (until recently) of 
Cyclicals.   
Any relief in bond markets, leading to lower yields, would hamper the relative 
performance of Cyclicals and Financials and favour some of the more defensive 
areas of the equity markets — another reason to have some exposure to selective 
defensives and also sectors that are highly negatively correlated with bond yields.  
Sovereign debt concerns could result in a further spike in bond yields, with the risks 
highest in Europe during an election-heavy year, while an alleviation of inflationary 
pressures might lead to a rally.  
2. Oil prices  
The combination of rising oil prices and bond yields has been central to the movement in 
equity markets through 2026. Our commodity analysts forecast oil at $88/bbl for Brent

Exhibit 18: EM devalued sharply through 2026 compared with DM

### Page 17

and $78/bbl for WTI in 2027, with risks in both directions. In general, lower energy
prices would be good for equity markets, bringing down inflation and interest rate
expectations. On balance, we think this would boost longer duration growth valuations
(from the relatively depressed levels currently), while also supporting defensive growth.
By contrast, such an outcome would reduce profit growth expectations for Energy
companies — a factor that would likely weigh more on Europe given that Energy is a

relatively important contributor to earnings growth.
3. AI capex spend
AI capex spending has been an important support for equity markets over the past three
years, driving earnings growth across the Technology ecosystem, and beyond into the
capex beneficiaries. Our economists expect the AI capex boom to slow. This could lead
to a de-rating in sectors that have become the beneficiaries of the capex boom,
including parts of the Industrials sector, which has re-rated, but also memory and chips

stocks. The latter have de-rated sharply, making the entry point more attractive, but
volatility is likely to remain high.
4. Narrow Breadth & Concentration
Related to this point, the US and Asia equity markets have a high degree of stock
concentration in their indices. Narrow breadth can create more stock volatility, with the
dominant stocks rising or the rest of the market falling. Either way, the momentum factor
risks high volatility and having some diversification across Growth and some Defensives

should help to improve risk-adjusted returns.  From a geographic perspective, the two
most concentrated regions are the US and Asia. That said, given recent market action,
we think the large cap technology names in the US are more defensive that those in Asia.
On a risk-adjusted basis, therefore, we think the momentum and concentration risks are
higher in Asia and imply lower risk-adjusted returns.
